## Q44. In India, deficit financing is used for raising resources for
**Primary route:** Economy → [Union Budget, Fiscal Policy and Deficit Indicators](../../topics/economy/union-budget-fiscal-policy-and-deficit-indicators.md) → In India, deficit financing is used for raising resources for
**Primary classification:** Economy → Union Budget, Fiscal Policy and Deficit Indicators → In India, deficit financing is used for raising resources for
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q44 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/09_Union-Budget-Fiscal-Policy-and-Deficit-Indicators.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/09_Union-Budget-Fiscal-Policy-and-Deficit-Indicators.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-in-india-deficit-financing-is-used-for-raising-resources-for`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In India, deficit financing is used for raising resources for

(a) economic development  
(b) redemption of public debt  
(c) adjusting the balance of payments  
(d) reducing the foreign debt  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — economic development**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Deficit financing is a fiscal tool where the government's total expenditure exceeds its total revenue (excluding borrowings), and the gap is covered through borrowing or the creation of new money. In India, the primary objective of deficit financing is to raise resources for **economic development** (Option 0). In developing economies, tax revenues are often insufficient to fund the massive investments required for infrastructure (roads, power, ports), social welfare, and poverty alleviation programs. Deficit financing allows the government to bridge this investment gap, stimulate aggregate demand, and utilize idle resources to boost national income and GDP growth (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 5; Nitin Singhania, Indian Economy). It is not used for the redemption of public debt or reducing foreign debt, as the process of deficit financing inherently increases the government's total debt burden (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics). Furthermore, it is generally not a tool for adjusting the balance of payments; in fact, excessive deficit financing can lead to inflation, making exports less competitive and potentially worsening the balance of payments (Ramesh Singh, Indian Economy).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — economic development. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: economic development** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: redemption of public debt** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: adjusting the balance of payments** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: reducing the foreign debt** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

## Q45. Which of the following characterizes/characterize the people of Indus Civilization? 1. They possessed...
**Primary route:** Ancient History → [Harappan Civilization](../../topics/ancient-history/harappan-civilization.md) → Which of the following characterizes/characterize the people of Indus Civilization? 1. They possessed...
**Primary classification:** Ancient History → Harappan Civilization → Which of the following characterizes/characterize the people of Indus Civilization? 1. They possessed...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q45 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Ancient-Indian-History/basic/06_Harappan-Civilization.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Ancient-Indian-History/basic/06_Harappan-Civilization.md)
**Canonical slug:** `ancient-history-which-of-the-following-characterizes-characterize-the-people-of-indus-civilization-1-they-possessed-great-palaces-and-te`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following characterizes/characterize the people of Indus Civilization?
1. They possessed great palaces and temples.
2. They worshipped both male and female deities.
3. They employed horse-drawn chariots in warfare.
Select the correct statement/statements using the codes given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 3  
(d) None of the statements given above is correct  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Indus Valley Civilization (IVC) is characterized by several distinct cultural and religious features, but the statements provided must be evaluated against archaeological evidence:

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: Unlike contemporary civilizations in Mesopotamia and Egypt, the Indus Valley Civilization has yielded no conclusive evidence of monumental temples or palaces. While large public buildings like the 'Great Bath' at Mohenjo-daro and various 'Granaries' have been excavated, their functions were likely ritualistic or administrative rather than residential for royalty or dedicated to specific deities in a temple setting (NCERT Class 12 History, Chapter 1; Khan Academy). 

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: The Harappan people worshipped both male and female deities. Evidence for female worship comes from numerous terracotta figurines identified as the 'Mother Goddess,' symbolizing fertility. Evidence for male worship is found in the 'Pashupati' seal, which depicts a three-faced figure (often called Proto-Shiva) seated in a yogic posture and surrounded by animals (NCERT Class 12 History, Chapter 1; Drishti IAS).

3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: Horse-drawn chariots were not a feature of the Indus Valley Civilization. While there is some debated evidence of horse remains (e.g., at Surkotada), the horse was not a central animal in Harappan life, and the use of horse-drawn chariots in warfare is a defining characteristic of the later Vedic period associated with the Indo-Aryans (NCERT Class 12 History, Chapter 1; canonical topic evidence). Harappan transportation primarily relied on bullock carts with solid wheels.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: None of the statements given above is correct** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following diseases can be transmitted from one person to another through tattooing?
1. Chikungunya
2. Hepatitis B
3. HIV-AIDS
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The transmission of diseases through tattooing occurs primarily when equipment (needles, ink, or gloves) is contaminated with infected blood. 
1. **Chikungunya**: This is a viral disease transmitted to humans by the bite of infected mosquitoes, primarily *Aedes aegypti* and *Aedes albopictus* (WHO). It is not considered a disease transmitted through tattooing in a public health context.
2. **Hepatitis B**: This is a blood-borne virus. The CDC and WHO identify tattooing with unsterilized or reused needles as a significant risk factor for Hepatitis B transmission because the virus can survive outside the body for long periods (CDC).
3. **HIV-AIDS**: HIV is transmitted through contact with infected blood. While the risk is lower than Hepatitis B, using needles or ink contaminated with HIV-infected blood during tattooing can transmit the virus (CDC).
Therefore, only Hepatitis B and HIV-AIDS are transmissible through tattooing. This aligns with the official UPSC answer key for the 2013 Civil Services Preliminary Examination.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 and 3 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. **Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements is/are applicable to Jain doctrine?
1. The surest way of annihilating Karma is to practice penance.
2. Every object, even the smallest particle has a soul.
3. Karma is the bane of the soul and must be ended.
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Jain doctrine is characterized by its unique perspective on karma, the soul, and the path to liberation. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: Jainism teaches that the accumulation of karma can be stopped (*samvara*) and existing karma can be shed or annihilated (*nirjara*). The primary method for *nirjara* is the practice of severe penance (*tapas*) and asceticism. According to NCERT Class 12 History, 'Asceticism and penance are required to free oneself from the cycle of karma' (NCERT Class 12, Themes in Indian History Part I, p. 88).

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: A fundamental tenet of Jainism is its animistic belief system. It posits that the entire world is animated and that souls (*jiva*) exist not only in humans and animals but also in plants, stones, rocks, and water. NCERT states, 'The most important idea in Jainism is that the entire world is animated: even stones, rocks and water have life' (NCERT Class 12, Themes in Indian History Part I, p. 88).

3. **Statement 3 is correct**: In Jain philosophy, karma is not merely a law of cause and effect but a subtle physical matter (*pudgala*) that flows into and sticks to the soul, weighing it down and obscuring its natural qualities of infinite knowledge and bliss. Thus, karma is viewed as the 'bane' or the cause of bondage for the soul, and the ultimate goal of life is to end this karmic attachment to achieve liberation (*moksha*).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following terms describes not only the physical space occupied by an organism, but also its functional role in the community of organisms?

(a) Ecotone  
(b) Ecological niche  
(c) Habitat  
(d) Home range  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Ecological niche**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The term 'Ecological niche' refers to the unique functional role and position of a species in its habitat or ecosystem. While a 'habitat' is often described as the 'address' of an organism (the physical space it occupies), the 'niche' is described as its 'profession' or 'job description' (its functional role). An ecological niche encompasses the physical space occupied by an organism, its interactions with other species (biotic factors), and its response to environmental conditions (abiotic factors) such as food sources, nesting behaviors, and trophic position. According to NCERT Class 12 Biology and other standard ecological texts, no two species can occupy the same niche in the same habitat for a long period (Competitive Exclusion Principle). In contrast, an 'Ecotone' is a transition zone between two ecosystems, 'Habitat' is the physical environment where an organism lives, and 'Home range' is the area an animal traverses for its normal activities like foraging and mating.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Ecological niche. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Ecotone** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: Ecological niche** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Habitat** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: Home range** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

## Q49. Photochemical smog is a resultant of the reaction among
**Primary route:** Environment → [Air Pollution and CPCB Standards](../../topics/environment/air-pollution-and-cpcb-standards.md) → Photochemical smog is a resultant of the reaction among
**Primary classification:** Environment → Air Pollution and CPCB Standards → Photochemical smog is a resultant of the reaction among
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q49 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/13_Air-Pollution-and-CPCB-Standards.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/13_Air-Pollution-and-CPCB-Standards.md)
**Canonical slug:** `environment-photochemical-smog-is-a-resultant-of-the-reaction-among`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Photochemical smog is a resultant of the reaction among

(a) NO2, O3 and peroxyacetyl nitrate in the presence of sunlight  
(b) CO, O2 and peroxyacetyl nitrate in the presence of sunlight  
(c) CO, CO2 and NO2 at low temperature  
(d) high concentration of NO2, O3 and CO in the evening  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — NO2, O3 and peroxyacetyl nitrate in the presence of sunlight**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Photochemical smog (also known as Los Angeles smog) is a type of air pollution that occurs in warm, dry, and sunny climates. It is formed through the reaction of primary pollutants like nitrogen oxides (NOx) and volatile organic compounds (VOCs) in the presence of sunlight. The primary pollutants undergo a series of photochemical reactions to produce secondary pollutants, which are the main constituents of the smog. These include Nitrogen Dioxide (NO2), Ozone (O3), and Peroxyacetyl Nitrate (PAN). According to NCERT Class 11 Chemistry (Chapter 14), the common components of photochemical smog are ozone, nitric oxide, acrolein, formaldehyde, and PAN. Because it contains high concentrations of oxidizing agents, it is also called oxidizing smog. In contrast, classical smog (London smog) occurs in cool, humid climates and consists of a mixture of smoke, fog, and sulfur dioxide (SO2).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — NO2, O3 and peroxyacetyl nitrate in the presence of sunlight. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: NO2, O3 and peroxyacetyl nitrate in the presence of sunlight** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: CO, O2 and peroxyacetyl nitrate in the presence of sunlight** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: CO, CO2 and NO2 at low temperature** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: high concentration of NO2, O3 and CO in the evening** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q50. Consider the following minerals: 1. Calcium 2. Iron 3. Sodium Which of the minerals given above is/are...
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Biology and Physiology](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-biology-and-physiology.md) → Consider the following minerals: 1. Calcium 2. Iron 3. Sodium Which of the minerals given above is/are...
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Biology and Physiology → Consider the following minerals: 1. Calcium 2. Iron 3. Sodium Which of the minerals given above is/are...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q50 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md)
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-consider-the-following-minerals-1-calcium-2-iron-3-sodium-which-of-the-minerals-given-above-is-are-required-by-human-bod`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following minerals:
1. Calcium
2. Iron
3. Sodium
Which of the minerals given above is/are required by human body for the contraction of muscles?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

All three minerals—calcium, iron, and sodium—are essential for the process of muscle contraction in the human body. 

1. **Calcium (1):** Calcium ions ($Ca^{2+}$) are the direct triggers for the mechanical contraction of muscles. According to the **Sliding Filament Theory**, when a nerve impulse reaches a muscle fiber, calcium is released from the sarcoplasmic reticulum. These ions bind to the protein troponin, causing a conformational change that moves tropomyosin away from the myosin-binding sites on actin filaments, allowing contraction to occur (NCERT Class 11 Biology, Chapter 20; StatPearls). 

2. **Sodium (3):** Sodium is critical for the electrical initiation of contraction. The transmission of a nerve impulse across the neuromuscular junction causes an influx of sodium ions into the muscle cell, leading to **depolarization**. This depolarization generates an action potential that travels along the sarcolemma and T-tubules, which is the prerequisite signal for the release of calcium (PMC - NIH; StatPearls). 

3. **Iron (2):** While not a direct signaling ion like calcium or sodium, iron is indispensable for muscle function as a core component of **myoglobin**. Myoglobin is an iron-containing protein in muscle cells that stores and transports oxygen. Since muscle contraction is an energy-intensive process requiring ATP produced via aerobic respiration, iron is necessary to provide the oxygen required for sustained muscle activity (NCERT Class 11 Biology, Chapter 20; Testbook). 

UPSC's official answer key for the 2013 Civil Services Preliminary Examination confirms that all three minerals are required.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q51 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliament.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliament.md)

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements: The Parliamentary Committee on Public Accounts
1. consists of not more than 25 Members of the Lok Sabha
2. scrutinizes appropriation and finance accounts of the Government
3. examines the report of the Comptroller and Auditor General of India
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Parliamentary Committee on Public Accounts (PAC) is one of the most important financial committees of the Indian Parliament. 
1. Statement 1 is incorrect: The Committee consists of 22 members, not 25. Out of these 22 members, 15 are elected by the Lok Sabha and 7 are elected by the Rajya Sabha from amongst their members according to the principle of proportional representation by means of a single transferable vote (M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter 23). 
2. Statement 2 is correct: The primary function of the PAC is to examine the appropriation accounts (which compare the actual expenditure with the sums granted by Parliament) and the finance accounts of the Government of India (Lok Sabha Secretariat, 'Committee on Public Accounts'). 
3. Statement 3 is correct: The PAC examines the audit reports of the Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG) of India. The CAG submits three audit reports to the President: audit report on appropriation accounts, audit report on finance accounts, and audit report on public undertakings. The PAC scrutinizes these reports to ensure that the money granted by Parliament has been spent within the scope of the demand (NCERT Class 11, Indian Constitution at Work, Chapter 5).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 and 3 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. ### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following Bhakti Saints:
1. Dadu Dayal
2. Guru Nanak
3. Tyagaraja
Who among the above was/were preaching when the Lodi dynasty fell and Babur took over?

(a) 1 and 3  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3  
(d) 1 and 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Lodi dynasty fell and Babur established the Mughal Empire following the First Battle of Panipat in 1526. Among the listed saints, only Guru Nanak (1469–1539) was active during this transition. He was a contemporary of the Lodi Sultans (Sikandar and Ibrahim Lodi) and witnessed Babur's invasions, which he poignantly described in his hymns known as 'Babur-vani' (NCERT Class 7 History, Chapter 8; NCERT Class 12 History, Chapter 6). Dadu Dayal (1544–1603) was born nearly two decades after the fall of the Lodi dynasty and was a contemporary of the Mughal Emperor Akbar. Tyagaraja (1767–1847) was a renowned composer of Carnatic music who lived much later, during the late 18th and early 19th centuries, coinciding with the Maratha rule in Thanjavur and the expansion of British influence in India (Britannica: Tyagaraja).

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **C: 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

## Q53. With reference to the food chains in ecosystems, which of the following kinds of organism is/are known...
**Primary route:** Environment → [Ecosystem Structure and Function](../../topics/environment/ecosystem-structure-and-function.md) → With reference to the food chains in ecosystems, which of the following kinds of organism is/are known...
**Primary classification:** Environment → Ecosystem Structure and Function → With reference to the food chains in ecosystems, which of the following kinds of organism is/are known...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q53 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/01_Ecosystem-Structure-and-Function.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/01_Ecosystem-Structure-and-Function.md)
**Canonical slug:** `environment-with-reference-to-the-food-chains-in-ecosystems-which-of-the-following-kinds-of-organism-is-are-known-as-decomposer-orga`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the food chains in ecosystems, which of the following kinds of organism is/are known as decomposer organism/organisms?
1. Virus
2. Fungi
3. Bacteria
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Decomposers, also known as saprotrophs, are organisms that break down dead or decaying organisms, performing the vital ecological role of nutrient recycling. 

1. **Fungi**: They are among the primary decomposers in most ecosystems. They secrete enzymes to break down complex organic matter, such as lignin and cellulose in wood, into simpler substances (NCERT Class 12 Biology, Chapter 14). 
2. **Bacteria**: Along with fungi, bacteria are the main decomposers. They break down organic waste and dead organisms into inorganic nutrients like nitrogen and phosphorus, which are then reused by plants (NCERT Class 10 Science, Chapter 15). 
3. **Viruses**: Viruses are not considered decomposers. They are obligate intracellular parasites that require a living host cell to replicate. They do not consume or break down dead organic matter for energy or nutrient cycling in the way saprotrophs do (NCERT Class 11 Biology, Chapter 2). 

Therefore, only fungi and bacteria are classified as decomposer organisms in the context of food chains.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 and 3 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q54. The most important fishing grounds of the world are found in the regions where
**Primary route:** Geography → [Oceans, Currents, Tides and Salinity](../../topics/geography/oceans-currents-tides-and-salinity.md) → The most important fishing grounds of the world are found in the regions where
**Primary classification:** Geography → Oceans, Currents, Tides and Salinity → The most important fishing grounds of the world are found in the regions where
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q54 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The most important fishing grounds of the world are found in the regions where

(a) warm and cold atmospheric currents meet  
(b) rivers drain out large amounts of freshwater into the sea  
(c) warm and cold oceanic currents meet  
(d) continental shelf is undulating  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — warm and cold oceanic currents meet**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The most important fishing grounds in the world are found in regions where warm and cold oceanic currents meet. This convergence leads to the mixing of waters with different temperatures and densities, which facilitates the upwelling of nutrient-rich water from the deeper ocean layers to the surface. These nutrients act as a fertilizer for phytoplankton, the primary producers in the marine food chain. An abundance of phytoplankton attracts large populations of fish, making these areas highly productive for commercial fishing. Notable examples include the Grand Banks off the coast of Newfoundland, where the warm Gulf Stream meets the cold Labrador Current, and the waters near Japan, where the warm Kuroshio Current meets the cold Oyashio Current (NCERT Class 11 Geography, Chapter 14). While shallow continental shelves (Option 3) are also productive, the meeting of currents is the specific geographical phenomenon associated with the world's most significant fishing grounds.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — warm and cold oceanic currents meet. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: warm and cold atmospheric currents meet** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: rivers drain out large amounts of freshwater into the sea** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: warm and cold oceanic currents meet** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: continental shelf is undulating** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q55. Which of the following is/are unique characteristic/characteristics of equatorial forests? 1. Presence...
**Primary route:** Geography → [Hot-Wet Equatorial Climate](../../topics/geography/hot-wet-equatorial-climate.md) → Which of the following is/are unique characteristic/characteristics of equatorial forests? 1. Presence...
**Primary classification:** Geography → Hot-Wet Equatorial Climate → Which of the following is/are unique characteristic/characteristics of equatorial forests? 1. Presence...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q55 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/15_Hot-Wet-Equatorial-Climate.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/15_Hot-Wet-Equatorial-Climate.md)
**Canonical slug:** `geography-which-of-the-following-is-are-unique-characteristic-characteristics-of-equatorial-forests-1-presence-of-tall-closely-set`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following is/are unique characteristic/characteristics of equatorial forests?
1. Presence of tall, closely set trees with crowns forming a continuous canopy
2. Coexistence of a large number of species
3. Presence of numerous varieties of epiphytes
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The equatorial forests, also known as tropical rainforests, exhibit several unique ecological characteristics due to the hot and wet climate (high temperature and heavy rainfall throughout the year). 
1. **Continuous Canopy:** Due to intense competition for sunlight, trees grow very tall (often 30–50 meters) and are closely spaced. Their crowns interlock to form a thick, continuous canopy that prevents most sunlight from reaching the forest floor, creating a distinct vertical stratification (G.C. Leong, Chapter 15; NCERT Class 11 Geography, Chapter 15). 
2. **High Biodiversity:** These forests are the most biodiverse terrestrial biomes on Earth. A single hectare can contain hundreds of different tree species coexisting together, unlike temperate forests where a few species dominate (Shankar IAS Environment, Chapter 3; G.C. Leong, Chapter 15). 
3. **Abundance of Epiphytes:** Epiphytes are plants that grow on other plants (usually trees) for physical support rather than nutrition. In the dense, shaded environment of the rainforest, numerous varieties of epiphytes like orchids, ferns, and bromeliads thrive by growing high up in the canopy to access sunlight and moisture from the air (G.C. Leong, Chapter 15; Britannica). 
Since all three statements accurately describe the unique features of equatorial forests, the correct answer is 1, 2, and 3.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

## Q56. Which of the following constitute Capital Account? 1. Foreign Loans 2. Foreign Direct Investment 3. Pr...
**Primary route:** Economy → [Balance of Payments, Exchange Rates and Forex Reserves](../../topics/economy/balance-of-payments-exchange-rates-and-forex-reserves.md) → Which of the following constitute Capital Account? 1. Foreign Loans 2. Foreign Direct Investment 3. Pr...
**Primary classification:** Economy → Balance of Payments, Exchange Rates and Forex Reserves → Which of the following constitute Capital Account? 1. Foreign Loans 2. Foreign Direct Investment 3. Pr...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q56 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/19_Balance-of-Payments-Exchange-Rates-and-Forex-Reserves.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/19_Balance-of-Payments-Exchange-Rates-and-Forex-Reserves.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-which-of-the-following-constitute-capital-account-1-foreign-loans-2-foreign-direct-investment-3-private-remittances-4-po`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following constitute Capital Account?
1. Foreign Loans
2. Foreign Direct Investment
3. Private Remittances
4. Portfolio Investment
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1, 2 and 3  
(b) 1, 2 and 4  
(c) 2, 3 and 4  
(d) 1, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1, 2 and 4**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Balance of Payments (BoP) is divided into two main accounts: the Current Account and the Capital Account. 

1. **Foreign Loans**: These are part of the **Capital Account**. They include external assistance, external commercial borrowings (ECBs), and short-term trade credits. These transactions create a future liability for the country (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 6).
2. **Foreign Direct Investment (FDI)**: This is a component of the **Capital Account**. It involves long-term capital investment where the investor has a significant degree of influence or control over the enterprise (RBI BoP Definitions).
3. **Private Remittances**: These are part of the **Current Account**. Remittances are classified under 'Invisibles' as 'Unilateral Transfers' because they are one-way payments (gifts, worker remittances) that do not result in a claim or liability (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 6).
4. **Portfolio Investment**: This is part of the **Capital Account**. It refers to investments in financial assets like stocks and bonds by foreign entities without gaining controlling ownership (Drishti IAS, BoP Constituents).

Since items 1, 2, and 4 belong to the Capital Account and item 3 belongs to the Current Account, the correct combination is 1, 2, and 4.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 1, 2 and 4. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: 1, 2 and 4** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q57. Consider the following historical places: 1. Ajanta Caves 2. Lepakshi Temple 3. Sanchi Stupa Which of...
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Painting Traditions](../../topics/art-and-culture/painting-traditions.md) → Consider the following historical places: 1. Ajanta Caves 2. Lepakshi Temple 3. Sanchi Stupa Which of...

**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q57 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination

**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-consider-the-following-historical-places-1-ajanta-caves-2-lepakshi-temple-3-sanchi-stupa-which-of-the-above-places-is-ar`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following historical places:
1. Ajanta Caves
2. Lepakshi Temple
3. Sanchi Stupa
Which of the above places is/are also known for mural paintings?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 3  
(d) None  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question asks to identify historical places known for mural paintings among Ajanta Caves, Lepakshi Temple, and Sanchi Stupa. 

1. **Ajanta Caves (Maharashtra):** These are world-renowned for their mural paintings (frescoes and tempera) dating from the 2nd century BCE to the 5th century CE. The paintings predominantly narrate the Jataka tales and scenes from the life of Buddha. They are considered masterpieces of Buddhist religious art (NCERT Class 11, An Introduction to Indian Art, Chapter 4).

2. **Lepakshi Temple (Andhra Pradesh):** The Veerabhadra Temple at Lepakshi is famous for its exquisite mural paintings from the Vijayanagara period (16th century). These murals, which adorn the ceilings and walls of the mandapas, depict scenes from the Ramayana, Mahabharata, and Puranas, including a colossal 24x18 feet painting of Veerabhadra Swamy (NCERT Class 11, An Introduction to Indian Art, Chapter 5).

3. **Sanchi Stupa (Madhya Pradesh):** While Sanchi is a premier Buddhist site, it is primarily known for its architecture and stone sculptures, particularly the intricate carvings on its four gateways (toranas). It is not traditionally categorized as a site known for mural paintings in the same way Ajanta or Lepakshi are (NCERT Class 11, An Introduction to Indian Art, Chapter 3).

Therefore, only Ajanta Caves and Lepakshi Temple are known for mural paintings.

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **B: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: None** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

## Q58. With reference to the history of philosophical thought in India, consider the following statements reg...
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Religion, Philosophy and Cultural Synthesis](../../topics/art-and-culture/religion-philosophy-and-cultural-synthesis.md) → With reference to the history of philosophical thought in India, consider the following statements reg...

**Canonical owner:** [Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/13_Religion-Philosophy-and-Cultural-Synthesis.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/13_Religion-Philosophy-and-Cultural-Synthesis.md)
**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-with-reference-to-the-history-of-philosophical-thought-in-india-consider-the-following-statements-regarding-sankhya-scho`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the history of philosophical thought in India, consider the following statements regarding Sankhya school:
1. Sankhya does not accept the theory of rebirth or transmigration of soul.
2. Sankhya holds that it is the self-knowledge that leads to liberation and not any exterior influence or agent.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Sankhya school is one of the six classical (Astika) schools of Indian philosophy. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: Sankhya philosophy explicitly accepts the theory of rebirth and the transmigration of the soul (Purusha). It maintains that the soul is caught in the cycle of birth and death (Samsara) due to its association with Prakriti (matter) and the resulting ignorance. Liberation is the end of this cycle. According to the 'Sankhya Karika' (the foundational text), the 'subtle body' (linga-sarira) migrates from one gross body to another until final liberation is achieved (Sankhya Karika 39-40).

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: Sankhya holds that liberation (Kaivalya) is attained through 'Viveka-jnana' or discriminative knowledge. This is the realization that the Self (Purusha) is entirely distinct and independent from the material world (Prakriti). In its classical form, Sankhya is non-theistic (Nirishvara Sankhya) and does not believe in a creator god or external agent as the cause of liberation; rather, it is the internal realization of the true nature of the self that leads to freedom (Sankhya Karika 64). 

Grounding references include the NIOS Culture material on Indian Philosophy and standard texts like 'A Critical Survey of Indian Philosophy' by Chandradhar Sharma.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q59. In the context of India, which of the following principles is/are implied institutionally in the parli...
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → In the context of India, which of the following principles is/are implied institutionally in the parli...
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → In the context of India, which of the following principles is/are implied institutionally in the parli...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q59 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliamentary-System.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliamentary-System.md)
**Canonical slug:** `polity-in-the-context-of-india-which-of-the-following-principles-is-are-implied-institutionally-in-the-parliamentary-government`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the context of India, which of the following principles is/are implied institutionally in the parliamentary government?
1. Members of the Cabinet are Members of the Parliament.
2. Ministers hold the office till they enjoy confidence in the Parliament.
3. Cabinet is headed by the Head of the State.
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The parliamentary system of government in India is based on the principle of cooperation and coordination between the legislative and executive organs. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: In India, the executive is a part of the legislature. According to Article 75(5) of the Constitution, a minister who is not a member of either House of Parliament for any period of six consecutive months shall at the expiration of that period cease to be a minister. This implies that members of the Cabinet must be Members of Parliament (M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter 12: Parliamentary System).

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: The bedrock of the parliamentary system is the principle of collective responsibility. Article 75(3) explicitly states that the Council of Ministers shall be collectively responsible to the Lok Sabha (the lower house of Parliament). This means the ministry stays in office only as long as it enjoys the confidence of the Lok Sabha (NCERT Class 11, Indian Constitution at Work, Chapter 4: Executive).

3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: In the Indian parliamentary system, the President is the 'Head of the State' (nominal executive), while the Prime Minister is the 'Head of the Government' (real executive). The Cabinet is headed by the Prime Minister, not the Head of the State (Article 74(1) of the Constitution of India).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 and 2 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q60. The annual range of temperature in the interior of the continents is high as compared to coastal areas...
**Primary route:** Geography → [Weather Elements](../../topics/geography/weather-elements.md) → The annual range of temperature in the interior of the continents is high as compared to coastal areas...
**Primary classification:** Geography → Weather Elements → The annual range of temperature in the interior of the continents is high as compared to coastal areas...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q60 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/13_Weather-Elements.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/13_Weather-Elements.md)
**Canonical slug:** `geography-the-annual-range-of-temperature-in-the-interior-of-the-continents-is-high-as-compared-to-coastal-areas-what-is-are-the-r`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The annual range of temperature in the interior of the continents is high as compared to coastal areas. What is/are the reason/reasons?
1. Thermal difference between land and water
2. Variation in altitude between continents and oceans
3. Presence of strong winds in the interior
4. Heavy rains in the interior as compared to coasts
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The high annual range of temperature in the interior of continents compared to coastal areas is primarily due to the 'continentality' effect, which is driven by the thermal difference between land and water (Statement 1). Land has a lower specific heat capacity than water, meaning it heats up and cools down much more rapidly. Furthermore, water is transparent, allowing solar radiation to penetrate to greater depths, and it is mobile, allowing heat to be distributed through convection and currents. In contrast, land is opaque and heat is concentrated on the surface (NCERT Class 11, Fundamentals of Physical Geography, Chapter 9). Statement 2 is incorrect because while altitude affects the absolute temperature (lapse rate), the difference in altitude between the continent and the ocean surface is not the cause of the seasonal temperature *range* difference. Statement 3 is incorrect as winds in the interior do not inherently cause a higher temperature range; coastal areas actually experience moderating sea and land breezes. Statement 4 is incorrect because coastal areas generally receive more precipitation than continental interiors, and moisture/rainfall typically has a moderating effect on temperature rather than increasing its range (PMF IAS, Factors Controlling Temperature Distribution).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: 1, 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

<a id="2013-prelims-gs1-q061"></a>
## Q61. Which of the following is/are the characteristic/characteristics of Indian coal? 1. High ash content 2...
**Primary route:** Geography → [Mineral and Energy Resources: World and India](../../topics/geography/mineral-energy-resources-world-and-india.md) → Which of the following is/are the characteristic/characteristics of Indian coal? 1. High ash content 2...
**Primary classification:** Geography → Mineral and Energy Resources: World and India → Which of the following is/are the characteristic/characteristics of Indian coal? 1. High ash content 2...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q61 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/31_Mineral-Energy-Resources-World-and-India.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/31_Mineral-Energy-Resources-World-and-India.md)
**Canonical slug:** `geography-which-of-the-following-is-are-the-characteristic-characteristics-of-indian-coal-1-high-ash-content-2-low-sulphur-content`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following is/are the characteristic/characteristics of Indian coal?
1. High ash content
2. Low sulphur content
3. Low ash fusion temperature
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Indian coal, primarily sourced from Gondwana formations, has distinct physical and chemical properties. 1. **High Ash Content**: Indian coal is notorious for its high ash content, which typically ranges from 35% to 45%, and sometimes even higher. This is significantly higher than the 10-15% ash content found in imported coal from countries like Indonesia or Australia (Ministry of Coal). 2. **Low Sulphur Content**: A major environmental advantage of Indian coal is its low sulphur content, usually staying within the range of 0.2% to 0.7%. This results in lower sulphur dioxide emissions during combustion compared to high-sulphur coals found in other parts of the world (PIB). 3. **Ash Fusion Temperature**: Statement 3 is incorrect because Indian coal is characterized by a **high** ash fusion temperature (AFT), often exceeding 1500°C. A high AFT is beneficial as it prevents the ash from melting and forming 'clinkers' or slag on the boiler walls, though it necessitates specific boiler designs for efficient combustion (NITI Aayog). Therefore, only statements 1 and 2 are correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 and 2 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

## Q62. Which of the following statements regarding laterite soils of India are correct? 1. They are generally...
**Primary route:** Geography → [Weathering, Mass Movement and Groundwater](../../topics/geography/weathering-massmovement-groundwater.md) → Which of the following statements regarding laterite soils of India are correct? 1. They are generally...
**Primary classification:** Geography → Weathering, Mass Movement and Groundwater → Which of the following statements regarding laterite soils of India are correct? 1. They are generally...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q62 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/04_Weathering-MassMovement-Groundwater.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/04_Weathering-MassMovement-Groundwater.md)
**Canonical slug:** `geography-which-of-the-following-statements-regarding-laterite-soils-of-india-are-correct-1-they-are-generally-red-in-colour-2-the`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements regarding laterite soils of India are correct?
1. They are generally red in colour.
2. They are rich in nitrogen and potash.
3. They are well-developed in Rajasthan and UP.
4. Tapioca and cashew nuts grow well on these soils.
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1, 2 and 3  
(b) 2, 3 and 4  
(c) 1 and 4  
(d) 2 and 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 4**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Based on the characteristics of Indian soils as described in NCERT and other standard geography texts: 
1. **Statement 1 is correct**: Laterite soils are generally red in colour due to the presence of iron oxides (hematite and limonite) resulting from intense leaching (NCERT Class 11 Geography, Chapter 6). 
2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: Laterite soils are formed under conditions of high temperature and heavy rainfall, which leads to intense leaching. This process makes the soil poor in nitrogen, phosphate, calcium, and organic matter. While some sources mention potash may be present, the soil is definitely deficient in nitrogen, making the statement 'rich in nitrogen and potash' incorrect (NCERT Class 11 Geography, Chapter 6; Majid Husain, Geography of India). 
3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: Laterite soils are well-developed in areas of high rainfall and high temperature, such as the Western Ghats, Eastern Ghats, Rajmahal Hills, and parts of Odisha, Kerala, and Karnataka. Rajasthan is dominated by arid soils, and Uttar Pradesh is dominated by alluvial soils (NCERT Class 11 Geography, Chapter 6). 
4. **Statement 4 is correct**: Red laterite soils in states like Tamil Nadu, Andhra Pradesh, and Kerala are particularly suitable for tree crops like cashew nuts and tapioca (NCERT Class 10 Geography, Chapter 1). 
Therefore, only statements 1 and 4 are correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1 and 4. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: 1 and 4** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. Natural gas occurs in the Gondwana beds.
2. Mica occurs in abundance in Kodarma.
3. Dharwars are famous for petroleum.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3  
(d) None  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: The Gondwana system is primarily known for its extensive coal deposits, containing nearly 98% of India's coal reserves. While Coal Bed Methane (CBM) is found in coal seams, natural gas as a major resource is associated with Tertiary sedimentary basins (NCERT Class 12 Geography, Chapter 7). Statement 2 is correct: Kodarma in Jharkhand is a major center for mica production and is part of the Bihar-Hazaribagh mica belt, which was once the world's leading producer of high-quality muscovite mica (NCERT Class 10 Geography, Chapter 5). Statement 3 is incorrect: The Dharwar system consists of the oldest metamorphosed sedimentary rocks in India and is famous for metallic minerals like iron ore, manganese, copper, and gold. Petroleum is found in Tertiary rocks, such as those in the Brahmaputra valley, Gujarat coast, and offshore basins like Mumbai High (NCERT Class 12 Geography, Chapter 7).

<a id="2013-prelims-gs1-q064"></a>
## Q64. Consider the following crops: 1. Cotton 2. Groundnut 3. Rice 4. Wheat Which of these are Kharif crops?
**Primary route:** Geography → [Primary Economic Activities: Agriculture](../../topics/geography/primary-economic-activities-agriculture.md) → Consider the following crops: 1. Cotton 2. Groundnut 3. Rice 4. Wheat Which of these are Kharif crops?
**Primary classification:** Geography → Primary Economic Activities: Agriculture → Consider the following crops: 1. Cotton 2. Groundnut 3. Rice 4. Wheat Which of these are Kharif crops?
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q64 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/30_Primary-Economic-Activities-Agriculture.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/30_Primary-Economic-Activities-Agriculture.md)
**Canonical slug:** `geography-consider-the-following-crops-1-cotton-2-groundnut-3-rice-4-wheat-which-of-these-are-kharif-crops`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following crops:
1. Cotton
2. Groundnut
3. Rice
4. Wheat
Which of these are Kharif crops?

(a) 1 and 4  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 3  
(d) 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

In India, crops are primarily classified into three seasons: Kharif, Rabi, and Zaid. 

1. **Kharif Crops**: These are monsoon crops sown with the onset of the southwest monsoon (June–July) and harvested in autumn (September–October). They require high temperatures and abundant rainfall. Examples include **Rice**, **Cotton**, **Groundnut**, Maize, Jowar, Bajra, Tur (Arhar), Moong, Urad, Jute, and Soybean (NCERT Class 10 Geography, Chapter 4; NCERT Class 9 Science, Chapter 12). 
2. **Rabi Crops**: These are winter crops sown in October–December and harvested in April–June. They require a cool growing season and bright sunshine at the time of ripening. Examples include **Wheat**, Barley, Peas, Gram, and Mustard (NCERT Class 10 Geography, Chapter 4). 
3. **Zaid Crops**: These are short-duration crops grown during the summer months (March–June) between the Rabi and Kharif seasons, such as watermelon, muskmelon, and cucumber.

Based on this classification:
- **Cotton (1)** is a Kharif crop.
- **Groundnut (2)** is a Kharif crop.
- **Rice (3)** is a major Kharif crop.
- **Wheat (4)** is a Rabi crop.

Therefore, crops 1, 2, and 3 are Kharif crops, making Option 2 the correct answer.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Primary classification:** Geography → Hot and Mid-Latitude Desert Climate → "Climate is extreme, rainfall is scanty and the people used to be nomadic herders." The above statemen...

**Canonical slug:** `geography-climate-is-extreme-rainfall-is-scanty-and-the-people-used-to-be-nomadic-herders-the-above-statement-best-describes-which`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

"Climate is extreme, rainfall is scanty and the people used to be nomadic herders." The above statement best describes which of the following regions?

(a) African Savannah  
(b) Central Asian Steppe  
(c) North American Prairie  
(d) Siberian Tundra  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Central Asian Steppe**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The statement describes the Central Asian Steppe, a temperate grassland region. 1. **Climate is extreme**: The Steppes experience a 'continental climate' characterized by extreme temperature variations—very hot summers and bitterly cold winters (GC Leong, Chapter 18). 2. **Rainfall is scanty**: Precipitation is light, typically between 25 cm and 50 cm (10-20 inches) annually, which is enough for grass but not for dense forests (NCERT Class 7, Chapter 6). 3. **Nomadic Herders**: Historically, this region was the domain of nomadic tribes like the Kirghiz, Kazakhs, and Mongols. These groups practiced nomadic pastoralism, moving their livestock (horses, sheep, and cattle) across vast distances in search of seasonal pastures (GC Leong, Chapter 18). While the African Savannah also has nomadic herders (like the Maasai), its climate is tropical with distinct wet/dry seasons rather than 'extreme' continental temperature ranges. The North American Prairies have been largely converted to commercial grain farming, and the Siberian Tundra is characterized by permafrost and reindeer herding in a polar climate.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Central Asian Steppe. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

- **B: Central Asian Steppe** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: North American Prairie** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: Siberian Tundra** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
