## Q66. Consider the following statements: 1. Inflation benefits the debtors. 2. Inflation benefits the bondho...
**Primary route:** Economy → [RBI, Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management](../../topics/economy/rbi-monetary-policy-and-liquidity-management.md) → Consider the following statements: 1. Inflation benefits the debtors. 2. Inflation benefits the bondho...
**Primary classification:** Economy → RBI, Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management → Consider the following statements: 1. Inflation benefits the debtors. 2. Inflation benefits the bondho...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q66 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/03_Inflation-Price-Indices-and-Business-Cycles.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/03_Inflation-Price-Indices-and-Business-Cycles.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-consider-the-following-statements-1-inflation-benefits-the-debtors-2-inflation-benefits-the-bondholders-which-of-the-sta`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. Inflation benefits the debtors.
2. Inflation benefits the bondholders.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Inflation refers to the general increase in prices and the subsequent fall in the purchasing power of money. 1. Statement 1 is correct: Inflation benefits debtors (borrowers). When inflation occurs, the real value of money decreases. Since the nominal value of the debt remains fixed, the debtor repays the loan with money that has less purchasing power than the money they originally borrowed. Effectively, the 'real' burden of the debt is reduced (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics; Ramesh Singh, Indian Economy). 2. Statement 2 is incorrect: Inflation hurts bondholders (lenders). Bondholders receive fixed interest payments and a fixed principal amount at maturity. Inflation erodes the real value of these future cash flows. Additionally, as inflation rises, central banks often increase interest rates, which leads to a decrease in the market price of existing bonds (Investopedia, 'How Inflation Affects Borrowers and Lenders'). Therefore, only statement 1 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

<a id="2013-prelims-gs1-q067"></a>
## Q67. Disguised unemployment generally means
**Primary route:** Economy → [Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend](../../topics/economy/employment-labour-codes-skills-and-demographic-dividend.md) → Disguised unemployment generally means
**Primary classification:** Economy → Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend → Disguised unemployment generally means
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q67 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-disguised-unemployment-generally-means`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Disguised unemployment generally means

(a) large number of people remain unemployed  
(b) alternative employment is not available  
(c) marginal productivity of labour is zero  
(d) productivity of workers is low  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — marginal productivity of labour is zero**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Disguised unemployment is a phenomenon where more people are employed in an activity than are actually needed to maintain the current level of production. In such a situation, the 'marginal productivity of labour' is zero because the addition or removal of a worker does not change the total output. This concept is most prevalent in the agricultural sector of developing countries like India, where family members work on small landholdings even when fewer people could perform the same tasks efficiently (NCERT Class 10 Economics, Chapter 2). According to NCERT Class 11 Economics (Chapter 7), this occurs when people are visibly employed but are actually underemployed, as their contribution to the total production is negligible. If these surplus workers were moved to other sectors, the total agricultural production would remain unaffected, highlighting that their marginal contribution is zero.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — marginal productivity of labour is zero. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: large number of people remain unemployed** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: alternative employment is not available** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: marginal productivity of labour is zero** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: productivity of workers is low** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Primary classification:** Polity → Prime Minister and Council of Ministers → Consider the following statements: 1. The Council of Ministers in the Centre shall be collectively res...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q68 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/PM-and-Council-of-Ministers.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/PM-and-Council-of-Ministers.md)
**Canonical slug:** `polity-consider-the-following-statements-1-the-council-of-ministers-in-the-centre-shall-be-collectively-responsible-to-the-parl`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Council of Ministers in the Centre shall be collectively responsible to the Parliament.
2. The Union Ministers shall hold the office during the pleasure of the President of India.
3. The Prime Minister shall communicate to the President about the proposals for legislation.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect because Article 75(3) of the Constitution of India specifically states that the Council of Ministers shall be collectively responsible to the 'House of the People' (Lok Sabha), not the 'Parliament' as a whole. Parliament consists of the President, the Lok Sabha, and the Rajya Sabha; however, the executive is not collectively responsible to the Rajya Sabha. Statement 2 is correct as per Article 75(2), which mandates that 'The Ministers shall hold office during the pleasure of the President.' This means a minister can be removed by the President on the advice of the Prime Minister at any time. Statement 3 is correct according to Article 78(a), which defines the duties of the Prime Minister to include communicating to the President all decisions of the Council of Ministers relating to the administration of the affairs of the Union and proposals for legislation.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 and 3 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

## Q69. Consider the following statements: 1. National Development Council is an organ of the Planning Commiss...
**Primary route:** Polity → [Ministries and Departments of Government](../../topics/polity/ministries-and-departments-of-government.md) → Consider the following statements: 1. National Development Council is an organ of the Planning Commiss...
**Primary classification:** Polity → Ministries and Departments of Government → Consider the following statements: 1. National Development Council is an organ of the Planning Commiss...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q69 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination

**Canonical slug:** `polity-consider-the-following-statements-1-national-development-council-is-an-organ-of-the-planning-commission-2-the-economic-a`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. National Development Council is an organ of the Planning Commission.
2. The Economic and Social Planning is kept in the Concurrent List in the Constitution of India.
3. The Constitution of India prescribes that Panchayats should be assigned the task of preparation of plans for economic development and social justice.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: The National Development Council (NDC) was established in August 1952 as an apex body for decision-making and deliberations on development matters in India. While it worked closely with the Planning Commission, it was an independent advisory body chaired by the Prime Minister and included Chief Ministers of all states and Union Ministers. It was not an 'organ' (internal structural unit) of the Planning Commission itself (M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter on NITI Aayog/Planning Commission). Statement 2 is correct: 'Economic and social planning' is listed as Entry 20 in the Concurrent List (List III) of the Seventh Schedule of the Constitution of India. This allows both the Union and State governments to legislate on planning matters (Constitution of India, Seventh Schedule, List III). Statement 3 is correct: Article 243G of the Constitution, introduced by the 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992, mandates that State Legislatures may, by law, endow Panchayats with the power and authority to prepare plans for economic development and social justice (Constitution of India, Article 243G; PIB Release on Powers of Panchayati Raj Institutions).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 and 3 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

<a id="2013-prelims-gs1-q070"></a>
## Q70. Consider the following statements: 1. The Chairman and the Deputy Chairman of the Rajya Sabha are not...
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → Consider the following statements: 1. The Chairman and the Deputy Chairman of the Rajya Sabha are not...
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → Consider the following statements: 1. The Chairman and the Deputy Chairman of the Rajya Sabha are not...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q70 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliament.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliament.md)
**Canonical slug:** `polity-consider-the-following-statements-1-the-chairman-and-the-deputy-chairman-of-the-rajya-sabha-are-not-the-members-of-that`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Chairman and the Deputy Chairman of the Rajya Sabha are not the members of that House.
2. While the nominated members of the two Houses of the Parliament have no voting right in the presidential election, they have the right to vote in the election of the Vice President.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect because while the Chairman of the Rajya Sabha (the Vice-President of India) is not a member of the House (Article 66(2)), the Deputy Chairman is elected by the Rajya Sabha from amongst its own members (Article 89(2)). Therefore, the Deputy Chairman must be a member of the Rajya Sabha. Statement 2 is correct. According to Article 54 of the Constitution, the electoral college for the Presidential election consists only of the elected members of both Houses of Parliament and the elected members of the Legislative Assemblies of the States. Nominated members do not have the right to vote in the Presidential election. However, for the election of the Vice-President, Article 66(1) specifies that the electoral college consists of all members of both Houses of Parliament, which includes both elected and nominated members.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to National Legal Services Authority, consider the following statements:
1. Its objective is to provide free and competent legal services to the weaker sections of the society on the basis of equal opportunity.
2. It issues guidelines for the State Legal Services Authorities to implement the legal programmes and schemes throughout the country.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The National Legal Services Authority (NALSA) was constituted under the Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987, to give effect to the constitutional mandate of Article 39A, which provides for free legal aid to the poor and weaker sections of society and ensures justice for all. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: The primary objective of NALSA is to provide free and competent legal services to the weaker sections of society (such as women, children, SC/ST members, and industrial workmen) to ensure that opportunities for securing justice are not denied to any citizen by reason of economic or other disabilities (Section 12 of the Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987; NALSA Official Website). 

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: NALSA is the apex body that lays down policies, principles, and guidelines for the State Legal Services Authorities (SLSAs) to implement legal services programs and schemes throughout the country. It also monitors and evaluates the implementation of these programs and distributes funds to SLSAs and NGOs for legal aid initiatives (Section 4 of the Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987; Department of Justice, India).

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

## Q72. During a thunderstorm, the thunder in the skies is produced by the 1. meeting of cumulonimbus clouds i...
**Primary route:** Geography → [Weather Elements](../../topics/geography/weather-elements.md) → During a thunderstorm, the thunder in the skies is produced by the 1. meeting of cumulonimbus clouds i...
**Primary classification:** Geography → Weather Elements → During a thunderstorm, the thunder in the skies is produced by the 1. meeting of cumulonimbus clouds i...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q72 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/13_Weather-Elements.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/13_Weather-Elements.md)
**Canonical slug:** `geography-during-a-thunderstorm-the-thunder-in-the-skies-is-produced-by-the-1-meeting-of-cumulonimbus-clouds-in-the-sky-2-lightnin`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

During a thunderstorm, the thunder in the skies is produced by the
1. meeting of cumulonimbus clouds in the sky
2. lightning that separates the nimbus clouds
3. violent upward movement of air and water particles
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3  
(c) 1 and 3  
(d) None of the above produces the thunder  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — None of the above produces the thunder**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Thunder is the sound produced by the rapid expansion and contraction of air surrounding a lightning bolt. According to the National Weather Service (NOAA), a lightning strike can heat the air to approximately 30,000°C (54,000°F), which is five times hotter than the surface of the sun. This sudden, extreme heating causes the air to expand explosively, creating a sonic shock wave that we perceive as thunder. Statement 1 (meeting of cumulonimbus clouds) is a common misconception and does not produce thunder. Statement 2 (lightning separating nimbus clouds) is scientifically inaccurate. Statement 3 (violent upward movement of air and water particles) describes the convective process and 'updrafts' that lead to the formation of cumulonimbus clouds and the separation of electrical charges (leading to lightning), but it is not the direct cause of the sound of thunder itself. Therefore, none of the given statements correctly identify the cause of thunder (NCERT Class 11, Fundamentals of Physical Geography, Chapter 10).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — None of the above produces the thunder. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: None of the above produces the thunder** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

## Q73. Consider the following pairs: Tribe : State 1. Limboo (Limbu) : Sikkim 2. Karbi : Himachal Pradesh 3....
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Crafts, Textiles, Folk and Tribal Traditions](../../topics/art-and-culture/crafts-textiles-folk-and-tribal-traditions.md) → Consider the following pairs: Tribe : State 1. Limboo (Limbu) : Sikkim 2. Karbi : Himachal Pradesh 3....
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Crafts, Textiles, Folk and Tribal Traditions → Consider the following pairs: Tribe : State 1. Limboo (Limbu) : Sikkim 2. Karbi : Himachal Pradesh 3....
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q73 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/12_Crafts-Textiles-Folk-and-Tribal-Traditions.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/12_Crafts-Textiles-Folk-and-Tribal-Traditions.md)
**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-consider-the-following-pairs-tribe-state-1-limboo-limbu-sikkim-2-karbi-himachal-pradesh-3-dongaria-kondh-odisha-4-bonda`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs:
**Tribe : State**
1. Limboo (Limbu) : Sikkim
2. Karbi : Himachal Pradesh
3. Dongaria Kondh : Odisha
4. Bonda : Tamil Nadu
Which of the above pairs are correctly matched?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 and 4 only  
(c) 1, 3 and 4 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct pairs are 1 and 3. 

1. **Limboo (Limbu)**: This tribe is indigenous to the Himalayan regions and is primarily found in the state of **Sikkim** and the Darjeeling/Kalimpong districts of West Bengal. They are recognized as a Scheduled Tribe in Sikkim. 
2. **Karbi**: The Karbi people are a major ethnic group in Northeast India, predominantly residing in the **Karbi Anglong** and West Karbi Anglong districts of **Assam**. They are not native to Himachal Pradesh. 
3. **Dongaria Kondh**: They are a sub-group of the Kondh tribe and are designated as a Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Group (PVTG). They inhabit the Niyamgiri hill range in the Rayagada and Kalahandi districts of **Odisha**. 
4. **Bonda**: The Bonda (or Bondo) tribe is an ancient Austroasiatic tribal community and a PVTG residing in the isolated hill regions of the Malkangiri district in **Odisha**, not Tamil Nadu. 

Therefore, only pairs 1 and 3 are correctly matched.

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **C: 1, 3 and 4 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: 1, 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q74. Consider the following liquid assets: 1. Demand deposits with the banks 2. Time deposits with the bank...
**Primary route:** Economy → [Money Market, Capital Market and Financial Instruments](../../topics/economy/money-market-capital-market-and-financial-instruments.md) → Consider the following liquid assets: 1. Demand deposits with the banks 2. Time deposits with the bank...
**Primary classification:** Economy → Money Market, Capital Market and Financial Instruments → Consider the following liquid assets: 1. Demand deposits with the banks 2. Time deposits with the bank...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q74 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/07_Money-Market-Capital-Market-and-Financial-Instruments.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/07_Money-Market-Capital-Market-and-Financial-Instruments.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-consider-the-following-liquid-assets-1-demand-deposits-with-the-banks-2-time-deposits-with-the-banks-3-savings-deposits`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following liquid assets:
1. Demand deposits with the banks
2. Time deposits with the banks
3. Savings deposits with the banks
4. Currency
The correct sequence of these assets in the decreasing order of liquidity is

(a) 1-4-3-2  
(b) 4-3-2-1  
(c) 2-3-1-4  
(d) 4-1-3-2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 4-1-3-2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Liquidity refers to the ease and speed with which an asset can be converted into cash or used for transactions without a significant loss in value. 
1. **Currency (4)**: This is the most liquid asset as it is already in the form of cash and is universally accepted as a medium of exchange for immediate transactions (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 3).
2. **Demand Deposits (1)**: These are deposits in bank accounts (like current accounts) that can be withdrawn by the depositor at any time without prior notice. They are highly liquid and can be used for transactions via checks or electronic transfers (RBI Monetary Aggregates; NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 3).
3. **Savings Deposits (3)**: While similar to demand deposits, savings accounts often have certain restrictions on the frequency or amount of withdrawals, making them slightly less liquid than pure demand deposits (Rau's IAS Compass; Testbook).
4. **Time Deposits (2)**: Also known as Fixed Deposits (FDs), these have a specific maturity period. Withdrawing funds before maturity usually incurs a penalty and requires a formal process, making them the least liquid among the four (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 3).

Therefore, the correct decreasing order of liquidity is Currency (4) > Demand deposits (1) > Savings deposits (3) > Time deposits (2), which corresponds to the sequence 4-1-3-2.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 4-1-3-2. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1-4-3-2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: 4-3-2-1** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 2-3-1-4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: 4-1-3-2** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

(a) borrowing by scheduled banks from the RBI  
(b) lending by commercial banks to industry and trade  
(c) purchase and sale of government securities by the RBI  
(d) None of the above  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — purchase and sale of government securities by the RBI**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

In the Indian economy, Open Market Operations (OMO) refer to the purchase and sale of government securities (G-Secs) and treasury bills by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) to regulate the money supply in the banking system. When the RBI wants to increase the liquidity (money supply) in the economy, it purchases government securities from the market, thereby injecting cash into the system. Conversely, when the RBI aims to curb inflation or reduce excess liquidity, it sells government securities, which absorbs money from the market. OMO is a quantitative monetary policy tool used by the central bank to influence interest rates and maintain price stability. This definition is distinct from the 'Repo Rate' (the rate at which RBI lends to banks) or 'Bank Rate' (the rate for long-term borrowing), which are covered under other monetary instruments (Source: RBI Monetary Policy Instruments; NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 3).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — purchase and sale of government securities by the RBI. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: borrowing by scheduled banks from the RBI** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: lending by commercial banks to industry and trade** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: purchase and sale of government securities by the RBI** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: None of the above** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

<a id="2013-prelims-gs1-q076"></a>
## Q76. Priority Sector Lending by banks in India constitutes the lending to
**Primary route:** Economy → [Banking Structure, NBFCs and Financial Regulation](../../topics/economy/banking-structure-nbfcs-and-financial-regulation.md) → Priority Sector Lending by banks in India constitutes the lending to
**Primary classification:** Economy → Banking Structure, NBFCs and Financial Regulation → Priority Sector Lending by banks in India constitutes the lending to
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q76 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/05_Banking-Structure-NBFCs-and-Financial-Regulation.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/05_Banking-Structure-NBFCs-and-Financial-Regulation.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-priority-sector-lending-by-banks-in-india-constitutes-the-lending-to`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Priority Sector Lending by banks in India constitutes the lending to

(a) agriculture  
(b) micro and small enterprises  
(c) weaker sections  
(d) All of the above  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — All of the above**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Priority Sector Lending (PSL) is a mandate by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) that requires commercial banks to allocate a specific portion of their lending to sectors that are considered vital for the development of the country but may not receive adequate credit from the market. According to the RBI Master Directions on Priority Sector Lending, the eligible categories include: (i) Agriculture, (ii) Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises (MSMEs), (iii) Export Credit, (iv) Education, (v) Housing, (vi) Social Infrastructure, (vii) Renewable Energy, and (viii) Others (which includes Weaker Sections). Specifically, 'Agriculture' and 'Micro and Small Enterprises' are major pillars of the PSL framework with dedicated sub-targets (e.g., 18% for Agriculture and 7.5% for Micro Enterprises for domestic commercial banks). 'Weaker Sections' is also a distinct target category within PSL, encompassing small and marginal farmers, artisans, SC/STs, and individual women beneficiaries among others. Therefore, all the options provided are part of Priority Sector Lending in India.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — All of the above. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: agriculture** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: micro and small enterprises** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: weaker sections** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: All of the above** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q77. Which one among the following industries is the maximum consumer of water in India?

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one among the following industries is the maximum consumer of water in India?

(a) Engineering  
(b) Paper and pulp  
(c) Textiles  
(d) Thermal power  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Thermal power**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Thermal power plants are the largest industrial consumers of water in India. According to data from the Centre for Science and Environment (CSE) and various government reports, the thermal power sector accounts for approximately 88% of the total industrial water consumption in the country. Water is primarily used in these plants for cooling purposes (in condensers) and for steam generation. While other industries like engineering (approx. 5.05%), paper and pulp (approx. 2.26%), and textiles (approx. 2.07%) are also water-intensive, their total consumption is significantly lower than that of the thermal power sector. NCERT Class 12 Geography (India: People and Economy) Chapter 6 also notes that within the industrial sector, thermal power plants are the major consumers of water.

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **C: Textiles** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: Thermal power** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

## Q78. To obtain full benefits of demographic dividend, what should India do?
**Primary route:** Economy → [Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend](../../topics/economy/employment-labour-codes-skills-and-demographic-dividend.md) → To obtain full benefits of demographic dividend, what should India do?
**Primary classification:** Economy → Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend → To obtain full benefits of demographic dividend, what should India do?
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q78 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-to-obtain-full-benefits-of-demographic-dividend-what-should-india-do`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

To obtain full benefits of demographic dividend, what should India do?

(a) Promoting skill development  
(b) Introducing more social security schemes  
(c) Reducing infant mortality rate  
(d) Privatization of higher education  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Promoting skill development**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 'demographic dividend' refers to the economic growth potential that can result from shifts in a population's age structure, specifically when the share of the working-age population (15 to 64 years) is larger than the non-working-age share (UNFPA). To obtain the full benefits of this dividend, India must ensure that its large working-age population is productive and employable. Promoting skill development (Option 0) is the most critical intervention because it bridges the gap between the available labor force and the requirements of the modern economy. The Economic Survey of India (e.g., 2011-12 and 2018-19) and the 12th Five Year Plan have consistently advocated for skill development as the primary tool to reap this dividend. While reducing the Infant Mortality Rate (IMR) and introducing social security schemes are vital for human development and welfare, they do not directly 'unlock' the economic productivity of the existing youth bulge as effectively as vocational training and skilling. Government initiatives like the 'Skill India Mission' and 'Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana' (PMKVY) are specifically designed to address this need by enhancing the employability of the youth (PIB, Economic Survey 2023-24).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — Promoting skill development. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

- **A: Promoting skill development** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: Introducing more social security schemes** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: Reducing infant mortality rate** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: Privatization of higher education** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q79. In the context of cultural history of India, a pose in dance and dramatics called 'Tribhanga' has been...
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Indian Dance](../../topics/art-and-culture/indian-dance.md) → In the context of cultural history of India, a pose in dance and dramatics called 'Tribhanga' has been...

**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-in-the-context-of-cultural-history-of-india-a-pose-in-dance-and-dramatics-called-tribhanga-has-been-a-favourite-of-india`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the context of cultural history of India, a pose in dance and dramatics called 'Tribhanga' has been a favourite of Indian artists from ancient times till today. Which one of the following statements best describes this pose?

(a) One leg is bent and the body is slightly but oppositely curved at waist and neck  
(b) Facial expressions, hand gestures and make-up are combined to symbolize certain epic or historic characters  
(c) Movements of body, face and hands are used to express oneself or to tell a story  
(d) A little smile, slightly curved waist and certain hand gestures are emphasized to express the feelings of love or eroticism  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — One leg is bent and the body is slightly but oppositely curved at waist and neck**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The term 'Tribhanga' literally translates to 'three bends' (Tri = three, Bhanga = bend/attitude). It is a signature standing posture in Indian classical dance, particularly Odissi, and is a recurring motif in traditional Indian sculpture and painting. The pose is characterized by three distinct deflections in the body's vertical alignment: at the knees, the waist (hips), and the neck. Specifically, one leg is bent at the knee, shifting the body's weight and causing the hips to curve in one direction, while the torso and neck curve in the opposite direction to maintain balance. This creates a graceful, asymmetrical 'S' shape silhouette. This posture is famously associated with the deity Krishna (often called 'Tribhanga Murari') and is considered a feminine and fluid stance compared to the masculine 'Chauka' (square) stance. References to this pose can be found in the Natya Shastra and Shilpa Shastras, and it is prominently discussed in NCERT textbooks on Indian Art.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — One leg is bent and the body is slightly but oppositely curved at waist and neck. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: One leg is bent and the body is slightly but oppositely curved at waist and neck** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: Facial expressions, hand gestures and make-up are combined to symbolize certain epic or historic characters** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: Movements of body, face and hands are used to express oneself or to tell a story** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: A little smile, slightly curved waist and certain hand gestures are emphasized to express the feelings of love or eroticism** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-annie-besant-was-1-responsible-for-starting-the-home-rule-movement-2-the-founder-of-the-theosophical-society-3-once-the`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Annie Besant was
1. responsible for starting the Home Rule Movement
2. the founder of the Theosophical Society
3. once the President of the Indian National Congress
Select the correct statement/statements using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Annie Besant was a prominent British socialist, theosophist, and supporter of Indian self-rule. 
1. **Statement 1 is correct**: Annie Besant was responsible for starting the Home Rule Movement in India. She launched the All India Home Rule League in September 1916 at Madras (now Chennai), following Bal Gangadhar Tilak, who had started his own league in April 1916. Her movement had an all-India character and aimed at achieving dominion status for India within the British Empire (Bipin Chandra, 'India's Struggle for Independence'; NCERT Class 12 History, Themes in Indian History Part III).
2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: Annie Besant was not the founder of the Theosophical Society. The society was founded in New York City in 1875 by Helena Petrovna Blavatsky and Colonel Henry Steel Olcott. Besant joined the society much later, in 1889, and became its President in 1907 after Olcott's death (Britannica; Theosophical Society Adyar official history).
3. **Statement 3 is correct**: Annie Besant was the first woman to be elected as the President of the Indian National Congress. She presided over the Calcutta session of the Congress in 1917 (Official Indian National Congress website; NCERT Class 12 History).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1 and 3 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

## Q81. The Ilbert Bill controversy was related to the
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Colonial Government Structure and Constitutional Development](../../topics/modern-history/colonial-government-structure-and-constitutional-development.md) → The Ilbert Bill controversy was related to the
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Colonial Government Structure and Constitutional Development → The Ilbert Bill controversy was related to the
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q81 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/12_Administrative-Changes-After-1858.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/12_Administrative-Changes-After-1858.md)
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-the-ilbert-bill-controversy-was-related-to-the`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The Ilbert Bill controversy was related to the

(a) imposition of certain restrictions to carry arms by the Indians  
(b) imposition of restrictions on newspapers and magazines published in Indian languages  
(c) removal of disqualifications imposed on the Indian magistrates with regard to the trial of the Europeans  
(d) removal of a duty on imported cotton cloth  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — removal of disqualifications imposed on the Indian magistrates with regard to the trial of the Europeans**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Ilbert Bill was introduced in 1883 during the viceroyalty of Lord Ripon. It was drafted by Sir Courtenay Ilbert, the Law Member of the Viceroy's Council. The bill sought to remove the judicial disqualification based on race, which prevented Indian magistrates and session judges from trying European British subjects in criminal cases in the mofussil (countryside) districts. Prior to this, only European judges could preside over such trials. The bill triggered a massive backlash from the European community in India, who formed the 'Defence Association' to protest against it, citing racial superiority. This controversy highlighted the deep-seated racial prejudices of the British administration and played a significant role in the growth of Indian nationalism, eventually leading to the formation of the Indian National Congress in 1885. A compromise was later reached in 1884, which allowed Indian magistrates to try Europeans but gave the accused the right to demand a jury where at least half the members were Europeans or Americans (NCERT Class 8 History, Chapter 9; Spectrum: A Brief History of Modern India).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — removal of disqualifications imposed on the Indian magistrates with regard to the trial of the Europeans. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: imposition of certain restrictions to carry arms by the Indians** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: imposition of restrictions on newspapers and magazines published in Indian languages** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: removal of disqualifications imposed on the Indian magistrates with regard to the trial of the Europeans** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: removal of a duty on imported cotton cloth** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

A rise in the general level of prices, known as inflation, is caused by an imbalance between the supply of money and the supply of goods and services. 1. **Increase in the money supply**: According to the Quantity Theory of Money (MV = PT), if the velocity of money (V) and the volume of transactions (T) are constant, an increase in the money supply (M) leads directly to an increase in the price level (P). This is often described as 'too much money chasing too few goods' (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 3). 2. **Decrease in the aggregate level of output**: If the total production of goods and services in an economy falls while demand remains constant or grows, the resulting scarcity forces prices upward. This is known as supply-shock inflation (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 4). 3. **Increase in the effective demand**: Demand-pull inflation occurs when the aggregate demand for goods and services exceeds the economy's capacity to produce them. Factors such as increased government spending, tax cuts, or low interest rates can boost effective demand, leading to higher prices (Ramesh Singh, Indian Economy, Chapter on Inflation). Therefore, all three factors contribute to a rise in the general level of prices.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q83. Which one of the following groups of items is included in India's foreign-exchange reserves?
**Primary route:** Economy → [Balance of Payments, Exchange Rates and Forex Reserves](../../topics/economy/balance-of-payments-exchange-rates-and-forex-reserves.md) → Which one of the following groups of items is included in India's foreign-exchange reserves?
**Primary classification:** Economy → Balance of Payments, Exchange Rates and Forex Reserves → Which one of the following groups of items is included in India's foreign-exchange reserves?
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q83 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/19_Balance-of-Payments-Exchange-Rates-and-Forex-Reserves.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/19_Balance-of-Payments-Exchange-Rates-and-Forex-Reserves.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-which-one-of-the-following-groups-of-items-is-included-in-indias-foreign-exchange-reserves`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following groups of items is included in India's foreign-exchange reserves?

(a) Foreign-currency assets, Special Drawing Rights (SDRs) and loans from foreign countries  
(b) Foreign-currency assets, gold holdings of the RBI and SDRs  
(c) Foreign-currency assets, loans from the World Bank and SDRs  
(d) Foreign-currency assets, gold holdings of the RBI and loans from the World Bank  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Foreign-currency assets, gold holdings of the RBI and SDRs**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

India's foreign-exchange reserves (Forex Reserves) are managed by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) and consist of four primary components: (1) Foreign Currency Assets (FCA), which include holdings of major global currencies like the US Dollar, Euro, and Pound Sterling; (2) Gold holdings of the RBI; (3) Special Drawing Rights (SDRs) with the International Monetary Fund (IMF); and (4) Reserve Tranche Position (RTP) in the IMF. Option 1 correctly identifies three of these four components. Loans from foreign countries or international organizations like the World Bank (mentioned in other options) are considered liabilities and are part of the 'External Debt' or 'Capital Account' of the Balance of Payments, but they are not included in the official foreign-exchange reserves. According to the RBI's Weekly Statistical Supplement and NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, these reserves act as a buffer to manage exchange rate volatility and ensure external payment obligations are met.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Foreign-currency assets, gold holdings of the RBI and SDRs. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Foreign-currency assets, Special Drawing Rights (SDRs) and loans from foreign countries** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: Foreign-currency assets, gold holdings of the RBI and SDRs** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Foreign-currency assets, loans from the World Bank and SDRs** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: Foreign-currency assets, gold holdings of the RBI and loans from the World Bank** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

<a id="2013-prelims-gs1-q084"></a>
## Q84. Which one of the following is likely to be the most inflationary in its effect?
**Primary route:** Economy → [Union Budget, Fiscal Policy and Deficit Indicators](../../topics/economy/union-budget-fiscal-policy-and-deficit-indicators.md) → Which one of the following is likely to be the most inflationary in its effect?
**Primary classification:** Economy → Union Budget, Fiscal Policy and Deficit Indicators → Which one of the following is likely to be the most inflationary in its effect?
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q84 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/09_Union-Budget-Fiscal-Policy-and-Deficit-Indicators.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/09_Union-Budget-Fiscal-Policy-and-Deficit-Indicators.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-which-one-of-the-following-is-likely-to-be-the-most-inflationary-in-its-effect`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following is likely to be the most inflationary in its effect?

(a) Repayment of public debt  
(b) Borrowing from the public to finance a budget deficit  
(c) Borrowing from banks to finance a budget deficit  
(d) Creating new money to finance a budget deficit  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Creating new money to finance a budget deficit**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Creating new money to finance a budget deficit, also known as the 'monetization of the deficit,' is the most inflationary method among the choices. When the government borrows from the central bank (RBI in India) and the bank prints new currency to fund this, it directly increases the 'High Powered Money' or 'Reserve Money' (M0) in the economy. This new money enters the banking system and, through the money multiplier effect, leads to a significant expansion of the total money supply (M3). Since this increase in money supply often occurs without a corresponding increase in the production of goods and services, it leads to 'demand-pull inflation' (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 5). 

In contrast:
- **Repayment of public debt (Option 0):** This involves the government returning money to the public, often funded by tax revenue, which is generally neutral or even deflationary as it reduces the government's future interest obligations.
- **Borrowing from the public (Option 1):** This is a transfer of existing purchasing power from the private sector to the government. It does not increase the net money supply and may lead to 'crowding out' of private investment, which can actually be anti-inflationary (Rau's IAS Compass).
- **Borrowing from banks (Option 2):** While this can lead to some credit expansion, it is limited by statutory requirements like the Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR) and Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR), making it less inflationary than direct money creation.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — Creating new money to finance a budget deficit. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Repayment of public debt** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: Borrowing from the public to finance a budget deficit** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: Borrowing from banks to finance a budget deficit** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: Creating new money to finance a budget deficit** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

## Q85. Supply of money remaining the same when there is an increase in demand for money, there will be
**Primary route:** Economy → [RBI, Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management](../../topics/economy/rbi-monetary-policy-and-liquidity-management.md) → Supply of money remaining the same when there is an increase in demand for money, there will be
**Primary classification:** Economy → RBI, Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management → Supply of money remaining the same when there is an increase in demand for money, there will be
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q85 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-supply-of-money-remaining-the-same-when-there-is-an-increase-in-demand-for-money-there-will-be`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Supply of money remaining the same when there is an increase in demand for money, there will be

(a) a fall in the level of prices  
(b) an increase in the rate of interest  
(c) a decrease in the rate of interest  
(d) an increase in the level of income and employment  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — an increase in the rate of interest**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

According to the Liquidity Preference Theory of interest developed by John Maynard Keynes, the interest rate is determined by the intersection of the supply of money and the demand for money. In this model, the money supply is typically considered to be fixed by the central bank (represented as a vertical line). If the supply of money remains constant and there is an increase in the demand for money (a rightward shift of the demand curve), the equilibrium interest rate will rise. This occurs because, at the initial interest rate, there is an excess demand for money; individuals and firms will attempt to obtain more cash by selling financial assets like bonds. As the selling pressure on bonds increases, bond prices fall, which is mathematically equivalent to an increase in the interest rate (NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics, Chapter 3). This mechanism ensures that the market returns to equilibrium at a higher rate of interest.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — an increase in the rate of interest. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: a fall in the level of prices** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: an increase in the rate of interest** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: a decrease in the rate of interest** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: an increase in the level of income and employment** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

<a id="2013-prelims-gs1-q086"></a>
## Q86. Fruits stored in a cold chamber exhibit longer storage life because
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Biology and Physiology](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-biology-and-physiology.md) → Fruits stored in a cold chamber exhibit longer storage life because
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Biology and Physiology → Fruits stored in a cold chamber exhibit longer storage life because
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q86 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md)
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-fruits-stored-in-a-cold-chamber-exhibit-longer-storage-life-because`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Fruits stored in a cold chamber exhibit longer storage life because

(a) exposure to sunlight is prevented  
(b) concentration of carbon dioxide in the environment is increased  
(c) rate of respiration is decreased  
(d) there is an increase in humidity  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — rate of respiration is decreased**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Fruits are living tissues that continue to respire even after being harvested. Respiration is a metabolic process where fruits break down stored organic substrates (like sugars and acids) to produce energy, carbon dioxide, and water. This process is driven by enzymes, whose activity is highly sensitive to temperature. In a cold chamber, the low temperature significantly reduces the activity of these respiratory enzymes, thereby decreasing the rate of respiration (NCERT Class 11 Biology, Chapter 14). A lower respiration rate slows down the ripening process and delays senescence (the aging and eventual decay of the fruit), which effectively extends its storage life. While other factors like humidity (to prevent moisture loss) and carbon dioxide levels (in Controlled Atmosphere Storage) are also important, the fundamental biological reason for extended shelf life in standard cold storage is the thermal suppression of the respiration rate (UPSC 2013; Post-harvest Technology principles).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — rate of respiration is decreased. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: exposure to sunlight is prevented** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: concentration of carbon dioxide in the environment is increased** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: rate of respiration is decreased** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: there is an increase in humidity** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following fauna of India:
1. Gharial
2. Leatherback turtle
3. Swamp deer
Which of the above is/are endangered?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 3  
(d) None  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

According to the official UPSC answer key for the 2013 Civil Services Preliminary Examination, all three species—Gharial, Leatherback turtle, and Swamp deer—are considered 'endangered' in the context of the question. 

1. **Gharial (Gavialis gangeticus)**: It is classified as **Critically Endangered** on the IUCN Red List. It is primarily found in the Chambal River and is listed under Schedule I of the Wildlife Protection Act, 1972 (WPA).
2. **Leatherback turtle (Dermochelys coriacea)**: At the time of the 2013 exam, its global IUCN status was **Critically Endangered** (it was downlisted to Vulnerable later in 2013). In India, it nests in the Andaman and Nicobar Islands and is protected under Schedule I of the WPA.
3. **Swamp deer (Rucervus duvaucelii)**: Also known as Barasingha, it is classified as **Vulnerable** on the IUCN Red List. However, it is listed under Schedule I of the WPA, which provides the highest level of legal protection in India. 

In UPSC examinations, the term 'endangered' is often used broadly to refer to species that are 'threatened' (including Critically Endangered, Endangered, and Vulnerable categories) or those that are highly protected under Indian law (Schedule I of WPA).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

- **C: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
