## Q88. Ball bearings are used in bicycles, cars, etc., because
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Physics Fundamentals](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-physics-fundamentals.md) → Ball bearings are used in bicycles, cars, etc., because
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Physics Fundamentals → Ball bearings are used in bicycles, cars, etc., because
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q88 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/21_General-Science-Physics-Fundamentals.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/21_General-Science-Physics-Fundamentals.md)
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-ball-bearings-are-used-in-bicycles-cars-etc-because`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Ball bearings are used in bicycles, cars, etc., because

(a) the actual area of contact between the wheel and axle is increased  
(b) the effective area of contact between the wheel and axle is increased  
(c) the effective area of contact between the wheel and axle is reduced  
(d) None of the above statements is correct  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — the effective area of contact between the wheel and axle is reduced**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Ball bearings are used in bicycles, cars, and other machinery to reduce friction by converting sliding friction into rolling friction. According to NCERT Class 8 Science (Chapter 12: Friction), rolling friction is significantly less than sliding friction. In a sliding mechanism (like a simple axle in a hub), the entire surface area of the axle is in contact with the hub, leading to high resistance. Ball bearings consist of spherical balls that touch the inner and outer races at minute points or very small elliptical areas. This design ensures that the 'effective area of contact' between the wheel and the axle is greatly reduced compared to sliding surfaces. By minimizing the contact area to these rolling points, the interlocking of surface irregularities is reduced, which significantly lowers the frictional force and improves mechanical efficiency (NCERT Class 11 Physics, Chapter 5: Laws of Motion). This specific question appeared in the UPSC Civil Services Prelims 2013 General Studies Paper I.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — the effective area of contact between the wheel and axle is reduced. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the actual area of contact between the wheel and axle is increased** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: the effective area of contact between the wheel and axle is increased** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: the effective area of contact between the wheel and axle is reduced** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: None of the above statements is correct** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/21_General-Science-Physics-Fundamentals.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/21_General-Science-Physics-Fundamentals.md)
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-consider-the-following-phenomena-1-size-of-the-sun-at-dusk-2-colour-of-the-sun-at-dawn-3-moon-being-visible-at-dawn-4-tw`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following phenomena:
1. Size of the sun at dusk
2. Colour of the sun at dawn
3. Moon being visible at dawn
4. Twinkle of stars in the sky
5. Polestar being visible in the sky
Which of the above are optical illusions?

(a) 1, 2 and 3  
(b) 3, 4 and 5  
(c) 1, 2 and 4  
(d) 2, 3 and 5  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1, 2 and 4**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question asks to identify which of the listed phenomena are optical illusions. 

1. **Size of the sun at dusk (Optical Illusion):** The sun appears larger when it is near the horizon compared to when it is high in the sky. This is primarily a psychological illusion known as the 'Moon Illusion' (or Sun Illusion), where the brain misinterprets the size of the object because it can be compared to terrestrial objects on the horizon. Additionally, atmospheric refraction flattens the sun's shape, contributing to its distorted appearance (NCERT Class 10 Science, Chapter 11).

2. **Colour of the sun at dawn (Optical Illusion):** The sun appears reddish at sunrise and sunset because sunlight must travel through a thicker layer of the atmosphere. Shorter wavelengths (blue and violet) are scattered away by atmospheric particles (Rayleigh scattering), leaving only the longer wavelengths (red) to reach the observer's eye. The sun itself is not red; this is an optical phenomenon (NCERT Class 10 Science, Chapter 11).

3. **Moon being visible at dawn (Physical Reality):** This is not an illusion. The moon is physically present in the sky at dawn during certain phases of its lunar cycle (from Full Moon to New Moon).

4. **Twinkle of stars in the sky (Optical Illusion):** Stars are distant point sources of light. As their light passes through the Earth's atmosphere, it undergoes continuous refraction due to varying air densities and temperatures. This causes the apparent position and intensity of the star to fluctuate rapidly, a phenomenon called stellar scintillation. The star itself does not twinkle (NCERT Class 10 Science, Chapter 11).

5. **Polestar being visible in the sky (Physical Reality):** The Polestar (Polaris) is a real celestial body visible in the sky due to its alignment with the Earth's axis of rotation. Its visibility is a physical fact, not an illusion.

Based on the official UPSC 2013 Answer Key, the correct phenomena are 1, 2, and 4.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1, 2 and 4. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: 3, 4 and 5** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: 1, 2 and 4** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 2, 3 and 5** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Physics Fundamentals → Rainbow is produced when sunlight falls on drops of rain. Which of the following physical phenomena ar...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q90 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Rainbow is produced when sunlight falls on drops of rain. Which of the following physical phenomena are responsible for this?
1. Dispersion
2. Refraction
3. Internal reflection
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The formation of a rainbow is a result of the interaction of sunlight with raindrops, involving three primary physical phenomena: refraction, dispersion, and internal reflection. When sunlight enters a raindrop, it is first refracted (bent) as it passes from the air into the water. During this process, the white light undergoes dispersion, splitting into its constituent colors (VIBGYOR) because different wavelengths of light bend at slightly different angles. These rays then strike the inner back surface of the raindrop and undergo internal reflection. Finally, the light is refracted again as it exits the raindrop and travels toward the observer's eye. According to NCERT Class 10 Science (Chapter 11, Section 11.4.1) and NCERT Class 12 Physics (Chapter 9, Section 9.7), the combined effect of refraction, dispersion, and internal reflection is what creates the spectrum of colors seen in a rainbow.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q91. Many transplanted seedlings do not grow because
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Biology and Physiology](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-biology-and-physiology.md) → Many transplanted seedlings do not grow because
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Biology and Physiology → Many transplanted seedlings do not grow because
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q91 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md)
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-many-transplanted-seedlings-do-not-grow-because`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Many transplanted seedlings do not grow because

(a) the new soil does not contain favourable minerals  
(b) most of the root hairs grip the new soil too hard  
(c) most of the root hairs are lost during transplantation  
(d) leaves get damaged during transplantation  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — most of the root hairs are lost during transplantation**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The primary reason many transplanted seedlings fail to grow is the loss of root hairs during the transplantation process. Root hairs are delicate, single-cell extensions of the root epidermal cells that significantly increase the surface area for the absorption of water and minerals from the soil (NCERT Class 11 Biology, Chapter 11). During transplantation, the physical disturbance of the soil around the roots often causes these fragile structures to break or be stripped away. Without sufficient root hairs, the seedling's ability to uptake water is severely compromised, leading to a condition known as 'transplant shock.' While the plant continues to lose water through transpiration from its leaves, it cannot absorb enough to compensate, often resulting in wilting or death (UPSC Prelims 2013; Testbook). Other factors like soil minerals or leaf damage are secondary and typically do not cause immediate failure if the root system remains functional.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — most of the root hairs are lost during transplantation. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the new soil does not contain favourable minerals** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: most of the root hairs grip the new soil too hard** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: most of the root hairs are lost during transplantation** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: leaves get damaged during transplantation** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q92. Economic growth in country X will necessarily have to occur if
**Primary route:** Economy → [Growth, Development, HDI, IHDI and MPI](../../topics/economy/growth-development-hdi-ihdi-and-mpi.md) → Economic growth in country X will necessarily have to occur if
**Primary classification:** Economy → Growth, Development, HDI, IHDI and MPI → Economic growth in country X will necessarily have to occur if
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q92 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/02_Growth-Development-HDI-IHDI-and-MPI.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/02_Growth-Development-HDI-IHDI-and-MPI.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-economic-growth-in-country-x-will-necessarily-have-to-occur-if`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Economic growth in country X will necessarily have to occur if

(a) there is technical progress in the world economy  
(b) there is population growth in X  
(c) there is capital formation in X  
(d) the volume of trade grows in the world economy  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — there is capital formation in X**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Economic growth is defined as a quantitative increase in the production of goods and services within an economy over a specific period. Capital formation, which involves the creation of physical assets like machinery, infrastructure, and equipment, directly enhances a nation's productive capacity (NCERT Class 11 Economics). According to fundamental economic models like the Harrod-Domar and Solow-Swan models, capital accumulation is a primary driver of growth because it allows for higher output per worker and expands the production possibility frontier. While technical progress in the world economy (Option 0) or growth in world trade (Option 3) can create favorable conditions, they do not guarantee growth in a specific country X unless that country has the internal capacity and infrastructure to utilize them. Similarly, population growth (Option 1) does not necessarily lead to economic growth; if not accompanied by sufficient investment, it can lead to a decline in per capita income and strain resources. Therefore, capital formation is the most fundamental internal driver that ensures an expansion of output, making it the necessary condition for economic growth in country X (Ramesh Singh, Indian Economy).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — there is capital formation in X. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: there is technical progress in the world economy** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: there is population growth in X** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: there is capital formation in X** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: the volume of trade grows in the world economy** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

## Q93. Which of the following statements is/are correct? 1. Viruses lack enzymes necessary for the generation...
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Biology and Physiology](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-biology-and-physiology.md) → Which of the following statements is/are correct? 1. Viruses lack enzymes necessary for the generation...

**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q93 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md)
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-which-of-the-following-statements-is-are-correct-1-viruses-lack-enzymes-necessary-for-the-generation-of-energy-2-viruses`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements is/are correct?
1. Viruses lack enzymes necessary for the generation of energy.
2. Viruses can be cultured in any synthetic medium.
3. Viruses are transmitted from one organism to another by biological vectors only.
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is '1 only' based on the biological characteristics of viruses as outlined in standard scientific literature and NCERT textbooks.

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: Viruses are obligate intracellular parasites. They lack their own metabolic machinery, including the enzymes necessary for the generation of energy (ATP) or the synthesis of proteins. They rely entirely on the host cell's ribosomes, enzymes, and nutrients to replicate. NCERT Class 11 Biology Chapter 2 notes that viruses lack 'energy storage or energy liberation systems' (NCERT Class 11 Biology, Ch 2).

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: Unlike bacteria or fungi, viruses cannot be grown on synthetic or artificial culture media (such as agar or nutrient broth). Because they require a living host's cellular machinery to replicate, they can only be cultured in living cells, embryonated eggs, or tissue cultures (Microbiology, Pelczar; NCERT Class 11 Biology, Ch 2).

3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: The use of the word 'only' makes this statement false. While many viruses are transmitted by biological vectors (e.g., mosquitoes for Dengue or Zika), they are also transmitted through various other pathways including respiratory droplets (Influenza, SARS-CoV-2), contaminated water or food (Hepatitis A, Norovirus), direct physical contact, and fomites (inanimate objects) (WHO; CDC; canonical topic evidence).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Primary classification:** Environment → Desertification, UNCCD and Land Degradation → Which of the following leaf modifications occurs/occur in desert areas to inhibit water loss? 1. Hard...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q94 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/03_Ecological-Succession-and-Biomes.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/03_Ecological-Succession-and-Biomes.md)
**Canonical slug:** `environment-which-of-the-following-leaf-modifications-occurs-occur-in-desert-areas-to-inhibit-water-loss-1-hard-and-waxy-leaves-2-ti`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following leaf modifications occurs/occur in desert areas to inhibit water loss?
1. Hard and waxy leaves
2. Tiny leaves or no leaves
3. Thorns instead of leaves
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Desert plants, also known as xerophytes, have evolved several morphological adaptations to survive in arid conditions by minimizing water loss (transpiration). 
1. **Hard and waxy leaves**: Many desert plants possess a thick, waxy cuticle on their leaf surfaces. This layer acts as a barrier to reduce the evaporation of water (NCERT Class 12 Biology, Chapter 13). 
2. **Tiny leaves or no leaves**: Reducing the size of leaves (microphylly) or the complete absence of leaves significantly decreases the surface area available for transpiration. In such plants, the stem often becomes green and takes over the function of photosynthesis (NCERT Class 6 Science, Chapter 9). 
3. **Thorns instead of leaves**: In plants like Opuntia (Cactus), leaves are modified into spines (thorns). This adaptation serves a dual purpose: it drastically reduces the surface area for water loss and protects the plant from herbivory (NCERT Class 11 Biology, Chapter 5). 
Since all three modifications are characteristic of desert vegetation to inhibit water loss, the correct answer is 1, 2, and 3.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The known forces of nature can be divided into four classes, viz., gravity, electromagnetism, weak nuclear force and strong nuclear force. With reference to them, which one of the following statements is not correct?

(a) Gravity is the strongest of the four  
(b) Electromagnetism acts only on particles with an electric charge  
(c) Weak nuclear force causes radioactivity  
(d) Strong nuclear force holds protons and neutrons inside the nucleus of an atom  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Gravity is the strongest of the four**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The statement 'Gravity is the strongest of the four' is incorrect. In reality, gravity is the weakest of the four fundamental forces of nature. According to NCERT Class 11 Physics (Chapter 1: Physical World), the relative strengths of the forces are as follows: Strong Nuclear Force (1), Electromagnetism (10^-2), Weak Nuclear Force (10^-13), and Gravitational Force (10^-39). 

1. Electromagnetism acts between particles with an electric charge and has an infinite range (NCERT Class 11 Physics, Chapter 1).
2. The weak nuclear force is responsible for certain nuclear processes such as beta decay, which is a form of radioactivity (Britannica, 'Weak Force').
3. The strong nuclear force is the most powerful force and is responsible for binding protons and neutrons together within the atomic nucleus, overcoming the enormous electric repulsion between protons (NCERT Class 11 Physics, Chapter 1).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — Gravity is the strongest of the four. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Gravity is the strongest of the four** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: Electromagnetism acts only on particles with an electric charge** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: Weak nuclear force causes radioactivity** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: Strong nuclear force holds protons and neutrons inside the nucleus of an atom** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

## Q96. The efforts to detect the existence of Higgs boson particle have become frequent news in the recent pa...
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Scientific Discoveries, Nobel Prizes and Scientists](../../topics/science-and-technology/scientific-discoveries-nobel-prizes-and-scientists.md) → The efforts to detect the existence of Higgs boson particle have become frequent news in the recent pa...
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Scientific Discoveries, Nobel Prizes and Scientists → The efforts to detect the existence of Higgs boson particle have become frequent news in the recent pa...
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q96 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/26_Scientific-Discoveries-Nobel-Prizes-and-Scientists.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/26_Scientific-Discoveries-Nobel-Prizes-and-Scientists.md)
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-the-efforts-to-detect-the-existence-of-higgs-boson-particle-have-become-frequent-news-in-the-recent-past-what-is-are-the`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The efforts to detect the existence of Higgs boson particle have become frequent news in the recent past. What is/are the importance/importances of discovering this particle?
1. It will enable us to understand as to why elementary particles have mass.
2. It will enable us in the near future to develop the technology of transferring matter from one point to another without traversing the physical space between them.
3. It will enable us to create better fuels for nuclear fission.
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Higgs boson, often referred to as the 'God particle', is a fundamental particle associated with the Higgs field. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: The primary importance of the Higgs boson is that it confirms the existence of the Higgs field, which permeates the universe and explains how elementary particles (like quarks and electrons) acquire mass through their interactions with this field (CERN, 'The Higgs boson'; Drishti IAS). Without this field, these particles would remain massless and travel at the speed of light, preventing the formation of atoms and complex matter.

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: The discovery of the Higgs boson does not enable the teleportation of physical matter. While 'quantum teleportation' is a real scientific concept, it refers to the transfer of quantum information (states) between particles using entanglement, not the physical movement of matter across space (canonical topic evidence, 'Quantum teleportation'). Furthermore, this technology is unrelated to the Higgs boson's role in mass generation.

3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: Nuclear fission involves the splitting of heavy atomic nuclei. The mass of a nucleus is primarily derived from the binding energy of the strong nuclear force (gluons), rather than the Higgs field, which only accounts for the mass of elementary particles (CERN; PMF IAS). There is no direct application of Higgs boson research in developing nuclear fission fuels.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option A. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Canonical slug:** `environment-mycorrhizal-biotechnology-has-been-used-in-rehabilitating-degraded-sites-because-mycorrhiza-enables-the-plants-to-1-resi`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Mycorrhizal biotechnology has been used in rehabilitating degraded sites because mycorrhiza enables the plants to
1. resist drought and increase absorptive area
2. tolerate extremes of pH
3. resist disease infestation
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Mycorrhizal biotechnology is a crucial tool in ecological restoration and the rehabilitation of degraded sites due to the symbiotic relationship between fungi and plant roots. 
1. **Resist drought and increase absorptive area**: Mycorrhizal fungi develop an extensive network of hyphae that extends far beyond the reach of the plant's own root hairs. This significantly increases the effective surface area for the absorption of water and essential nutrients (like phosphorus), thereby enhancing the plant's ability to survive in drought-prone or nutrient-poor soils (NCERT Class 12 Biology, Chapter 10; Source [1], [4]).
2. **Tolerate extremes of pH**: These fungi can buffer plants against environmental stresses such as soil toxicity and extreme pH levels (both acidic and alkaline). They facilitate nutrient uptake even in conditions where soil chemistry would otherwise make minerals unavailable to the plant (Source [3], [5]).
3. **Resist disease infestation**: Mycorrhizae act as a biological barrier against soil-borne pathogens. They can produce antimicrobial compounds, compete with harmful microbes for space and nutrients, and induce systemic resistance in the host plant, making it more resilient to disease (Source [1], [5], [7]).
Since all three statements accurately describe the benefits provided by mycorrhiza, the correct answer is 1, 2, and 3.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q98 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/NITI-Aayog.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/NITI-Aayog.md)
**Canonical slug:** `polity-who-among-the-following-constitute-the-national-development-council-1-the-prime-minister-2-the-chairman-finance-commissi`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Who among the following constitute the National Development Council?
1. The Prime Minister
2. The Chairman, Finance Commission
3. Ministers of the Union Cabinet
4. Chief Ministers of the States
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1, 2 and 3 only  
(b) 1, 3 and 4 only  
(c) 2 and 4 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1, 3 and 4 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The National Development Council (NDC), also known as the Rashtriya Vikas Parishad, was established on August 6, 1952, by an executive resolution of the Government of India. It serves as the apex body for decision-making and deliberations on development matters in India. According to the composition defined for the NDC, its members include: (1) The Prime Minister of India, who acts as the Chairman; (2) All Union Cabinet Ministers; (3) Chief Ministers of all States; (4) Chief Ministers/Administrators of all Union Territories; and (5) Members of the Planning Commission (now replaced by NITI Aayog). The Chairman of the Finance Commission is not a member of the NDC; the Finance Commission is a separate constitutional body established under Article 280 of the Constitution. Therefore, statements 1, 3, and 4 are correct, while statement 2 is incorrect.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 1, 3 and 4 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

- **A: 1, 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **B: 1, 3 and 4 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 2 and 4 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option B. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.

<a id="2013-prelims-gs1-q099"></a>
## Q99. The national income of a country for a given period is equal to the
**Primary route:** Economy → [National Income, GDP, GVA and Measurement](../../topics/economy/national-income-gdp-gva-and-measurement.md) → The national income of a country for a given period is equal to the
**Primary classification:** Economy → National Income, GDP, GVA and Measurement → The national income of a country for a given period is equal to the
**Metadata:** 2013 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q99 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/01_National-Income-GDP-GVA-and-Measurement.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/01_National-Income-GDP-GVA-and-Measurement.md)
**Canonical slug:** `economy-the-national-income-of-a-country-for-a-given-period-is-equal-to-the`
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The national income of a country for a given period is equal to the

(a) total value of goods and services produced by the nationals  
(b) sum of total consumption and investment expenditure  
(c) sum of personal income of all individuals  
(d) money value of final goods and services produced  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — money value of final goods and services produced**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

National Income is defined as the total monetary value of all final goods and services produced by the residents of a country during a specific period, typically a financial year. According to the NCERT Class 12 Macroeconomics textbook (Chapter 2: National Income Accounting), there are three primary methods to calculate this: the Product (Value Added) Method, the Income Method, and the Expenditure Method. The Product Method specifically defines National Income as the sum of the money value of all final goods and services produced, which prevents 'double counting' by excluding intermediate goods. 

- Option 0 (Total value produced by nationals) describes Gross National Product (GNP), but 'National Income' in a broad conceptual sense is best captured by the money value of final output.
- Option 1 is an incomplete version of the Expenditure Method, as it omits Government Expenditure (G) and Net Exports (X-M).
- Option 2 is incorrect because Personal Income differs from National Income; it excludes undistributed corporate profits and corporate taxes while including transfer payments.
- Option 3 is the standard definition used in introductory macroeconomics and is the correct answer according to the UPSC Prelims 2013 Official Answer Key.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — money value of final goods and services produced. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: total value of goods and services produced by the nationals** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **B: sum of total consumption and investment expenditure** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **C: sum of personal income of all individuals** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option D.
- **D: money value of final goods and services produced** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option D. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following grants/grant direct credit assistance to rural households?
1. Regional Rural Banks
2. National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development
3. Land Development Banks
Select the correct answer using the codes given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question asks for institutions providing 'direct' credit assistance to rural households. 
1. **Regional Rural Banks (RRBs):** Established under the RRB Act, 1976, these banks are specifically designed to provide direct credit to small and marginal farmers, agricultural laborers, and rural artisans (Source: RBI/NABARD). 
2. **National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development (NABARD):** NABARD is an apex development bank. Its primary function is to provide 'refinance' to other financial institutions like RRBs, Commercial Banks, and Cooperative Banks, which then lend to rural households. It does not provide direct credit assistance to individuals (Source: NABARD Official Website - 'About Us'). 
3. **Land Development Banks (LDBs):** These are part of the cooperative credit structure (now often called Cooperative Agriculture and Rural Development Banks). They provide long-term direct credit to farmers for purposes like land improvement, digging wells, and purchasing farm machinery (Source: NCERT Class 11 Economics, Chapter 6). 
Therefore, only RRBs and LDBs provide direct credit, making option 2 (1 and 3 only) the correct answer.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1 and 3 only. The numbered statements, pairs or alternatives resolve under the controlling explanation above; each competing code either includes a false item, excludes a true item, or asserts the wrong relationship.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the Commission final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts, includes an incorrect proposition, omits a required proposition, or states the wrong association relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Test every numbered statement or factual association independently, beginning with the decisive fact in the explanation. Retain only the code consistent with all verified items; here that yields option C. Elimination corroborates knowledge and is not treated as proof by itself.
**UPSC trap:** Watch for absolute qualifiers, reversed cause/effect, adjacent but wrong institutions or locations, and code choices made attractive by one true statement despite another false statement.
